## scene_clustering.py — key patterns
### discover_scenes_v6 (base function — all versions delegate here):
def discover_scenes_v6(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    min_magsac_inliers: int = MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    lgeu_topk: int = LGEU_TOPK,
    max_exhaustive_n: int = MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """v6 scene discovery: same as v5 with fallbacks for all failure modes.

    Differences from discover_scenes_v5:
    - Per-image SIFT extraction wrapped in try/except; failed images get None features.
    - LGEU pair selection wrapped in try/except; failure falls back to exhaustive pairs.
    - _match_pair wrapped in try/except per pair; failure counts as (0, 0).
    - Guard against empty pairs_to_match list.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    if len(names) < 2:
        return {n: "scene_0" for n in names}, {}

    if verbose:
        print(f"  [v6] extracting SIFT features for {len(names)} images ...")
    features: dict[str, Optional[_SiftFeatures]] = {}
    for name in names:
        try:
            features[name] = _extract_sift(image_paths[name])
        except Exception:
            features[name] = None

    # Select which pairs to SIFT-match
    use_lgeu = lgeu_model_payload is not None and len(names) > max_exhaustive_n
    if use_lgeu:
        try:
            pairs_to_match = _lgeu_select_pairs(
                image_paths, names, lgeu_model_payload, lgeu_topk, verbose
            )
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU selected {len(pairs_to_match)} / {len(names) * (len(names) - 1) // 2} pairs")
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU pair selection failed ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); falling back to exhaustive")
            pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))
    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v11 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v11(
    image_paths,
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v11: Lower Lowe-match threshold (30 vs 40) for exhaustive-path datasets.

    ETs uses exhaustive SIFT (N=22 < 40). 2 images are in 'outliers' because they
    have only 30-39 Lowe matches with scene_0 images (below the standard 40 threshold).
    Lowering to 30 may connect these outliers to scene_0, allowing COLMAP to register them.

    For large datasets (LGEU path, N > 40): standard threshold preserved.
    For small datasets (exhaustive path, N <= 40): threshold = 30.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    N = len(names)
    threshold = 30 if N <= MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N else good_match_threshold
    return discover_scenes_v6(
        image_paths,
        good_match_threshold=threshold,
        lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
        lgeu_topk=20,
        verbose=verbose,
    )



## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v11 SfM identical to v7 — uses lower Lowe threshold in scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v11 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7


## submission.py — build_submission_v9 signature (template)
def build_submission_v9(
    sample_path: Path,
    image_root: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    diagnostics_path: Path | None = None,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """v9 pipeline: GBM LGEU model (AUC 0.690) + LGEU_TOPK=20 (V7 conservative pairing).

    V8 used TOPK=30 which over-merged stairs sub-scenes, regressing from 27/73 (V7)
    to 22/73. V9 reverts to TOPK=20 while keeping GBM model quality for better pair
    ranking on hidden datasets.
    """
    sample = read_sample(sample_path)
    out_rows: list[dict] = []
    diagnostics: dict = {"datasets": {}, "version": 9}

    for dataset, group in sample.groupby("dataset", sort=False):
        image_paths: dict[str, Path] = {}
        scene_map: dict[str, str] = {}
        poses: dict[str, tuple] = {}
        sfm_info: dict = {}
        dataset_error: str | None = None

        try:
            for row in group.itertuples(index=False):
                path = image_root / "test" / dataset / row.image
                if path.is_file():
                    image_paths[row.image] = path

            if len(image_paths) >= 2:
                scene_map, _edge_data = discover_scenes_v9(
                    image_paths,
                    lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
                    verbose=verbose,
                )
                poses, sfm_info = run_sfm_per_scene_v9(
                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}
            poses = {}
            sfm_info = {"error":

### V12 — regression
Hypothesis: V12 Hypothesis: Video-aware temporal pair augmentation for scene clustering.

In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images end up in "outliers" with 0 cameras
registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40), so LGEU pair
selection is used instead of exhaustive SIFT. LGEU scores pairs by global descriptor
similarity and selects the top-20 neighbors per image; however, for a simultaneous
dual-camera video recording with overlapping timestamps, the LGEU model may misjudge
visual similarity and fail to select the temporally adjacent frames that provide the
strongest geometric overlap. When a frame's key neighbors are not among the LGEU-selected
pairs, that frame gets no valid SIFT edges and is stranded as an outlier — preventing
COLMAP from registering it. V12 supplements the LGEU-selected pairs with mandatory
sequential temporal neighbor pairs (±2 frames sorted by embedded filename timestamp) for
all video sequences (>=70% of filenames contain a 10-13 digit timestamp). This guarantees
each frame connects to its 1-2 nearest temporal neighbors before SIFT matching, regardless
of LGEU ranking. The extra pairs add at most 2*(N-1) additional SIFT comparisons (~88 for
stairs), a modest cost. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and potentially other
video hidden datasets) and a net increase in registered cameras over V9 without regressing
ETs or non-video datasets (which are unaffected by the new logic).
Result: total=23/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=4/51

## V9 Per-Scene Diagnostics (most informative)

Dataset: ETs (22 images)
  scene_0: 10/11 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  
  scene_1: 9/9 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  
  outliers: 0/2 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction

Dataset: stairs (51 images)
  scene_0: 3/15 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  
  scene_1: 0/2 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction
  outliers: 0/14 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction
  scene_2: 3/20 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  

## scene_clustering.py — key patterns
### discover_scenes_v6 (base function — all versions delegate here):
def discover_scenes_v6(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    min_magsac_inliers: int = MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    lgeu_topk: int = LGEU_TOPK,
    max_exhaustive_n: int = MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """v6 scene discovery: same as v5 with fallbacks for all failure modes.

    Differences from discover_scenes_v5:
    - Per-image SIFT extraction wrapped in try/except; failed images get None features.
    - LGEU pair selection wrapped in try/except; failure falls back to exhaustive pairs.
    - _match_pair wrapped in try/except per pair; failure counts as (0, 0).
    - Guard against empty pairs_to_match list.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    if len(names) < 2:
        return {n: "scene_0" for n in names}, {}

    if verbose:
        print(f"  [v6] extracting SIFT features for {len(names)} images ...")
    features: dict[str, Optional[_SiftFeatures]] = {}
    for name in names:
        try:
            features[name] = _extract_sift(image_paths[name])
        except Exception:
            features[name] = None

    # Select which pairs to SIFT-match
    use_lgeu = lgeu_model_payload is not None and len(names) > max_exhaustive_n
    if use_lgeu:
        try:
            pairs_to_match = _lgeu_select_pairs(
                image_paths, names, lgeu_model_payload, lgeu_topk, verbose
            )
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU selected {len(pairs_to_match)} / {len(names) * (len(names) - 1) // 2} pairs")
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU pair selection failed ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); falling back to exhaustive")
            pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))
    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v12 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v12(
    image_paths,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v12: Video-aware pair augmentation — guarantees temporal neighbors for video sequences.

    For video datasets (N > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N, timestamp filenames), LGEU selects only
    TOPK=20 pairs per image by global descriptor similarity. This can leave temporally
    isolated frames as 'outliers' when LGEU fails to select their key overlap pairs —
    the root cause of 14 stairs images getting 0 cameras registered in V9.

    V12 augments LGEU pairs with mandatory sequential neighbors (±2 frames by timestamp)
    for video sequences, ensuring each video frame connects to its nearest temporal
    neighbors before SIFT matching. Non-video and small datasets use standard V9 logic.
    """
    import re as _re

    names = sorted(image_paths)
    N = len(names)

    _ts_re = _re.compile(r"\d{10,13}")
    n_match = sum(1 for n in names if _ts_re.search(n))
    is_video = N >= 3 and n_match / N >= 0.7

    if N <= MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N or lgeu_model_payload is None or not is_video:
        return discover_scenes_v6(
            image_paths,
            lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
            lgeu_topk=20,
            verbose=verbose,
        )

    # Video sequence path: LGEU topk=20 + forced sequential temporal pairs
    def _ts_key(n):
        m = _ts_re.search(n)
        return int(m.group()) if m else 0

    sorted_by_time = sorted(names, key=_ts_key)

    try:
        lgeu_l

## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v11 SfM identical to v7 — uses lower Lowe threshold in scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v11 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v12 SfM identical to v7 — uses video-aware temporal pair augmentation in scene clustering
run_sfm_per_scene_v12 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

def build_submission_v9(

    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    diagnostics_path: Path | None = None,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> pd.DataFrame:

    V8 used TOPK=30 which over-merged stairs sub-scenes, regressing from 27/73 (V7)

    ranking on hidden datasets.

        image_paths: dict[str, Path] = {}
        scene_map: dict[str, str] = {}
        poses: dict[str, tuple] = {}
        sfm_info: dict = {}
        dataset_error: str | None = None

        try:
            for row in group.itertuples(index=False):
                path = image_root / "test" / dataset / row.image
                if path.is_file():
                    image_paths[row.image] = path

            if len(image_paths) >= 2:
                scene_map, _edge_data = discover_scenes_v9(
                    image_paths,
                    lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
                    verbose=verbose,
                )
                poses, sfm_info = run_sfm_per_scene_v9(
                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}
            poses = {}
            sfm_info = {"error":

## Recent Experiments (full details)

### V9 — regression
Hypothesis: GBM+TOPK20: V8 model quality (AUC=0.690) + V7 conservative pairing (TOPK=20). Preserves V7 scene splits while using better pair ranker. Expected: 27/73 public, better hidden.
Result: total=25/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=6/51

### V10 — regression
Hypothesis: GBM+TOPK25: midpoint grid search between V7 (TOPK=20, 27/73) and V8 (TOPK=30, 22/73). Tests whether TOPK=25 improves hidden dataset coverage without triggering stairs over-merge.
Result: total=22/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=3/51

### V11 — regression
Hypothesis: Lower Lowe threshold (30 vs 40) for exhaustive-path datasets (N<=40). ETs has 2 outlier images with 30-39 matches; lowering threshold may connect them to scene_0 for COLMAP registration.
Result: total=16/73, ETs=10/22, stairs=6/51

### V12 — regression
Hypothesis: V12 Hypothesis: Video-aware temporal pair augmentation for scene clustering.

In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images end up in "outliers" with 0 cameras
registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40), so LGEU pair
selection is used instead of exhaustive SIFT. LGEU scores pairs by global descriptor
similarity and selects the top-20 neighbors per image; however, for a simultaneous
dual-camera video recording with overlapping timestamps, the LGEU model may misjudge
visual similarity and fail to select the temporally adjacent frames that provide the
strongest geometric overlap. When a frame's key neighbors are not among the LGEU-selected
pairs, that frame gets no valid SIFT edges and is stranded as an outlier — preventing
COLMAP from registering it. V12 supplements the LGEU-selected pairs with mandatory
sequential temporal neighbor pairs (±2 frames sorted by embedded filename timestamp) for
all video sequences (>=70% of filenames contain a 10-13 digit timestamp). This guarantees
each frame connects to its 1-2 nearest temporal neighbors before SIFT matching, regardless
of LGEU ranking. The extra pairs add at most 2*(N-1) additional SIFT comparisons (~88 for
stairs), a modest cost. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and potentially other
video hidden datasets) and a net increase in registered cameras over V9 without regressing
ETs or non-video datasets (which are unaffected by the new logic).
Result: total=23/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=4/51

### V13 — smoke_error
Hypothesis: V13 Hypothesis: The LGEU GBM model's num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair
selection for video sequences like stairs. num_gap is computed as the minimum absolute
numeric difference between filename tokens divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, two frames ~600 ms apart (typical at ~1.6 fps effective rate for 51
frames over ~30 s) produce num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model associates high num_gap values
with cross-scene pairs (since same-scene non-video pairs often have small sequential
numeric indices), so it ranks these temporally distant video frame pairs with low
same-scene probability and excludes them from the TOPK=20 selection. This leaves
many video frames with no valid SIFT neighbors and strands them as outliers with zero
cameras registered — the failure mode observed in V9 (14 stairs outliers, 6/51 total).
V12 tried to patch this with temporal augmentation, but hurt scores (4/51) because
stairs has two simultaneous cameras whose timestamps interleave: adding ±2 sequential
temporal neighbors connects frames across camera streams, creating erroneous cross-stream
edges that collapse the two sub-scenes into one unresolvable cluster. V13 instead bypasses
the LGEU model entirely for video sequences and ranks pairs by raw cosine similarity of
global descriptors (top-20 per image). Cosine similarity captures visual appearance
directly without the timestamp artifact, is the natural distance metric the global
descriptor was trained under, and is robust to the dual-camera interleaved-timestamp
structure because it scores pairs by what they look like, not when they were captured.
Non-video datasets (ETs and all hidden structured-capture datasets) are unaffected and
use the standard V9 GBM+TOPK=20 path. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs and
a net increase in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).

    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v13 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v13(
    image_paths,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v13: Cosine-only pair ranking for video sequences — bypass LGEU num_gap penalty.

    For video datasets (N > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N, timestamp filenames), the LGEU model's
    num_gap feature is computed as min absolute numeric diff between filename tokens
    divided by 1000. For millisecond timestamps (~600ms gaps at normal video fps),
    this yields num_gap ≈ 0.6, which the GBM model treats as evidence of different
    scenes. This penalizes temporally distant but spatially overlapping frames,
    reducing pair recall and stranding frames as outliers.

    V13 bypasses the LGEU model for video sequences and ranks pairs by raw cosine
    similarity of global descriptors (TOPK=20 per image). Cosine similarity is the
    primary visual-appearance signal and avoids the timestamp artifact.
    Non-video and small datasets use standard V9 logic (GBM LGEU, TOPK=20).
    """
    import re as _re

    import numpy as np

    names = sorted(image_paths)
    N = len(names)

    _ts_re = _re.compile(r"\d{10,13}")
    n_match = sum(1 for n in names if _ts_re.search(n))
    is_video = N >= 3 and n_match / N >= 0.7

    if N <= MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N or lgeu_model_payload is None or not is_video:
        return discover_scenes_v6(
            image_paths,
            lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
            lgeu_topk=20,
            verbose=verbose,
        )

    # Video path: rank pairs 

## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

## submission.py — build_submission_v9 signature (template)
def build_submission_v9(
    sample_path: Path,
    image_root: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    diagnostics_path: Path | None = None,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """v9 pipeline: GBM LGEU model (AUC 0.690) + LGEU_TOPK=20 (V7 conservative pairing).

    V8 used TOPK=30 which over-merged stairs sub-scenes, regressing from 27/73 (V7)
    to 22/73. V9 reverts to TOPK=20 while keeping GBM model quality for better pair
    ranking on hidden datasets.
    """
    sample = read_sample(sample_path)
    out_rows: list[dict] = []
    diagnostics: dict = {"datasets": {}, "version": 9}

    for dataset, group in sample.groupby("dataset", sort=False):
        image_paths: dict[str, Path] = {}
        scene_map: dict[str, str] = {}
        poses: dict[str, tuple] = {}
        sfm_info: dict = {}
        dataset_error: str | None = None

        try:
            for row in group.itertuples(index=False):
                path = image_root / "test" / dataset / row.image
                if path.is_file():
                    image_paths[row.image] = path

            if len(image_paths) >= 2:
                scene_map, _edge_data = discover_scenes_v9(
                    image_paths,
                    lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
                    verbose=verbose,
                )
                poses, sfm_info = run_sfm_per_scene_v9(
                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}
            poses = {}
            sfm_info = {"error":

## Recent Experiments (full details)

### V9 — regression
Hypothesis: GBM+TOPK20: V8 model quality (AUC=0.690) + V7 conservative pairing (TOPK=20). Preserves V7 scene splits while using better pair ranker. Expected: 27/73 public, better hidden.
Result: total=25/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=6/51

### V10 — regression
Hypothesis: GBM+TOPK25: midpoint grid search between V7 (TOPK=20, 27/73) and V8 (TOPK=30, 22/73). Tests whether TOPK=25 improves hidden dataset coverage without triggering stairs over-merge.
Result: total=22/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=3/51

### V11 — regression
Hypothesis: Lower Lowe threshold (30 vs 40) for exhaustive-path datasets (N<=40). ETs has 2 outlier images with 30-39 matches; lowering threshold may connect them to scene_0 for COLMAP registration.
Result: total=16/73, ETs=10/22, stairs=6/51

### V12 — regression
Hypothesis: V12 Hypothesis: Video-aware temporal pair augmentation for scene clustering.

In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images end up in "outliers" with 0 cameras
registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40), so LGEU pair
selection is used instead of exhaustive SIFT. LGEU scores pairs by global descriptor
similarity and selects the top-20 neighbors per image; however, for a simultaneous
dual-camera video recording with overlapping timestamps, the LGEU model may misjudge
visual similarity and fail to select the temporally adjacent frames that provide the
strongest geometric overlap. When a frame's key neighbors are not among the LGEU-selected
pairs, that frame gets no valid SIFT edges and is stranded as an outlier — preventing
COLMAP from registering it. V12 supplements the LGEU-selected pairs with mandatory
sequential temporal neighbor pairs (±2 frames sorted by embedded filename timestamp) for
all video sequences (>=70% of filenames contain a 10-13 digit timestamp). This guarantees
each frame connects to its 1-2 nearest temporal neighbors before SIFT matching, regardless
of LGEU ranking. The extra pairs add at most 2*(N-1) additional SIFT comparisons (~88 for
stairs), a modest cost. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and potentially other
video hidden datasets) and a net increase in registered cameras over V9 without regressing
ETs or non-video datasets (which are unaffected by the new logic).
Result: total=23/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=4/51

### V13 — smoke_error
Hypothesis: V13 Hypothesis: The LGEU GBM model's num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair
selection for video sequences like stairs. num_gap is computed as the minimum absolute
numeric difference between filename tokens divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, two frames ~600 ms apart (typical at ~1.6 fps effective rate for 51
frames over ~30 s) produce num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model associates high num_gap values
with cross-scene pairs (since same-scene non-video pairs often have small sequential
numeric indices), so it ranks these temporally distant video frame pairs with low
same-scene probability and excludes them from the TOPK=20 selection. This leaves
many video frames with no valid SIFT neighbors and strands them as outliers with zero
cameras registered — the failure mode observed in V9 (14 stairs outliers, 6/51 total).
V12 tried to patch this with temporal augmentation, but hurt scores (4/51) because
stairs has two simultaneous cameras whose timestamps interleave: adding ±2 sequential
temporal neighbors connects frames across camera streams, creating erroneous cross-stream
edges that collapse the two sub-scenes into one unresolvable cluster. V13 instead bypasses
the LGEU model entirely for video sequences and ranks pairs by raw cosine similarity of
global descriptors (top-20 per image). Cosine similarity captures visual appearance
directly without the timestamp artifact, is the natural distance metric the global
descriptor was trained under, and is robust to the dual-camera interleaved-timestamp
structure because it scores pairs by what they look like, not when they were captured.
Non-video datasets (ETs and all hidden structured-capture datasets) are unaffected and
use the standard V9 GBM+TOPK=20 path. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs and
a net increase in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).

### V14 — regression
Hypothesis: V14 Hypothesis: For video sequences (N > 40, ≥70% timestamp filenames), the GBM LGEU model's
num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair selection. num_gap is computed as the minimum
absolute numeric token difference between two filenames divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, adjacent frames ~600 ms apart yield num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model was trained on
non-video datasets where same-scene pairs have small sequential file-index gaps (num_gap ≈ 0) and
cross-scene pairs have large gaps, so it learned to use high num_gap as a strong cross-scene signal.
Applied to video, this is a spurious artifact: the model assigns low same-scene probability to
temporally adjacent but spatially overlapping frames, causing them to miss the TOPK=20 cut and
end up as 'outliers' with no SIFT edges and zero cameras registered — exactly the failure mode
seen in V9 (14/51 stairs images stranded as outliers). Previous attempts to fix this were:
V12 (add ±2 temporal neighbor pairs), which hurt because stairs has two interleaved camera streams
whose cross-stream temporal neighbors are from different viewpoints; and V13 (bypass GBM entirely,
use cosine-only ranking), which crashed. V14 takes a more surgical approach: it runs the full GBM
model but zeros out num_gap before scoring for video sequences. This neutralizes the timestamp
artifact while preserving all six remaining discriminative features — cosine similarity, mutual
rank mean, mutual rank max, aspect_diff, area_ratio, and name_jaccard — which are all valid for
video and can distinguish same-scene from cross-scene pairs by visual appearance alone. Non-video
and small datasets (N ≤ 40) are unaffected and use standard V9 logic. We expect fewer outlier
images for stairs (and any other hidden video datasets), better scene coverage, and a net increase
in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).
Result: total=25/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=6/51

## V9 Per-Scene Diagnostics (most informative)

Dataset: ETs (22 images)
  scene_0: 10/11 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  
  scene_1: 9/9 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  
  outliers: 0/2 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction

Dataset: stairs (51 images)
  scene_0: 3/15 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  
  scene_1: 0/2 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction
  outliers: 0/14 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction
  scene_2: 3/20 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  

## scene_clustering.py — key patterns
### discover_scenes_v6 (base function — all versions delegate here):
def discover_scenes_v6(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    min_magsac_inliers: int = MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    lgeu_topk: int = LGEU_TOPK,
    max_exhaustive_n: int = MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """v6 scene discovery: same as v5 with fallbacks for all failure modes.

    Differences from discover_scenes_v5:
    - Per-image SIFT extraction wrapped in try/except; failed images get None features.
    - LGEU pair selection wrapped in try/except; failure falls back to exhaustive pairs.
    - _match_pair wrapped in try/except per pair; failure counts as (0, 0).
    - Guard against empty pairs_to_match list.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    if len(names) < 2:
        return {n: "scene_0" for n in names}, {}

    if verbose:
        print(f"  [v6] extracting SIFT features for {len(names)} images ...")
    features: dict[str, Optional[_SiftFeatures]] = {}
    for name in names:
        try:
            features[name] = _extract_sift(image_paths[name])
        except Exception:
            features[name] = None

    # Select which pairs to SIFT-match
    use_lgeu = lgeu_model_payload is not None and len(names) > max_exhaustive_n
    if use_lgeu:
        try:
            pairs_to_match = _lgeu_select_pairs(
                image_paths, names, lgeu_model_payload, lgeu_topk, verbose
            )
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU selected {len(pairs_to_match)} / {len(names) * (len(names) - 1) // 2} pairs")
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU pair selection failed ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); falling back to exhaustive")
            pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))
    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v14 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v14(
    image_paths,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v14: LGEU GBM scoring with num_gap neutralized for video sequences.

    The GBM model's num_gap feature = min(|token_a - token_b|) / 1000 across all
    numeric filename tokens. For millisecond Unix timestamps (~600ms between adjacent
    video frames at ~1.6 fps), num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model learned that large
    num_gap correlates with cross-scene pairs (training data used sequential file
    indices where same-scene pairs have small gaps). Applied to video, this penalizes
    temporally adjacent same-scene frames and strands them as 'outliers'.

    V14 zeros out num_gap before GBM scoring for video sequences (N > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    >=70% timestamp filenames). The model's 6 remaining features — cosine similarity,
    mutual rank mean/max, aspect_diff, area_ratio, name_jaccard — are all valid for
    video and continue to discriminate same-scene vs cross-scene pairs. Non-video and
    small datasets use standard V9 logic (full GBM, TOPK=20), unaffected.
    """
    import re as _re

    import numpy as np

    names = sorted(image_paths)
    N = len(names)

    _ts_re = _re.compile(r"\d{10,13}")
    n_match = sum(1 for n in names if _ts_re.search(n))
    is_video = N >= 3 and n_match / N >= 0.7

    if N <= MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N or lgeu_model_payload is None or not is_video:
        return discover_scenes_v6(
            image_paths,
            lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model

## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v11 SfM identical to v7 — uses lower Lowe threshold in scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v11 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

## submission.py — build_submission_v9 signature (template)
def build_submission_v9(
    sample_path: Path,
    image_root: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    diagnostics_path: Path | None = None,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """v9 pipeline: GBM LGEU model (AUC 0.690) + LGEU_TOPK=20 (V7 conservative pairing).

    V8 used TOPK=30 which over-merged stairs sub-scenes, regressing from 27/73 (V7)
    to 22/73. V9 reverts to TOPK=20 while keeping GBM model quality for better pair
    ranking on hidden datasets.
    """
    sample = read_sample(sample_path)
    out_rows: list[dict] = []
    diagnostics: dict = {"datasets": {}, "version": 9}

    for dataset, group in sample.groupby("dataset", sort=False):
        image_paths: dict[str, Path] = {}
        scene_map: dict[str, str] = {}
        poses: dict[str, tuple] = {}
        sfm_info: dict = {}
        dataset_error: str | None = None

        try:
            for row in group.itertuples(index=False):
                path = image_root / "test" / dataset / row.image
                if path.is_file():
                    image_paths[row.image] = path

            if len(image_paths) >= 2:
                scene_map, _edge_data = discover_scenes_v9(
                    image_paths,
                    lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
                    verbose=verbose,
                )
                poses, sfm_info = run_sfm_per_scene_v9(
                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}
            poses = {}
            sfm_info = {"error":

## Recent Experiments (full details)

### V10 — regression
Hypothesis: GBM+TOPK25: midpoint grid search between V7 (TOPK=20, 27/73) and V8 (TOPK=30, 22/73). Tests whether TOPK=25 improves hidden dataset coverage without triggering stairs over-merge.
Result: total=22/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=3/51

### V11 — regression
Hypothesis: Lower Lowe threshold (30 vs 40) for exhaustive-path datasets (N<=40). ETs has 2 outlier images with 30-39 matches; lowering threshold may connect them to scene_0 for COLMAP registration.
Result: total=16/73, ETs=10/22, stairs=6/51

### V12 — regression
Hypothesis: V12 Hypothesis: Video-aware temporal pair augmentation for scene clustering.

In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images end up in "outliers" with 0 cameras
registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40), so LGEU pair
selection is used instead of exhaustive SIFT. LGEU scores pairs by global descriptor
similarity and selects the top-20 neighbors per image; however, for a simultaneous
dual-camera video recording with overlapping timestamps, the LGEU model may misjudge
visual similarity and fail to select the temporally adjacent frames that provide the
strongest geometric overlap. When a frame's key neighbors are not among the LGEU-selected
pairs, that frame gets no valid SIFT edges and is stranded as an outlier — preventing
COLMAP from registering it. V12 supplements the LGEU-selected pairs with mandatory
sequential temporal neighbor pairs (±2 frames sorted by embedded filename timestamp) for
all video sequences (>=70% of filenames contain a 10-13 digit timestamp). This guarantees
each frame connects to its 1-2 nearest temporal neighbors before SIFT matching, regardless
of LGEU ranking. The extra pairs add at most 2*(N-1) additional SIFT comparisons (~88 for
stairs), a modest cost. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and potentially other
video hidden datasets) and a net increase in registered cameras over V9 without regressing
ETs or non-video datasets (which are unaffected by the new logic).
Result: total=23/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=4/51

### V13 — smoke_error
Hypothesis: V13 Hypothesis: The LGEU GBM model's num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair
selection for video sequences like stairs. num_gap is computed as the minimum absolute
numeric difference between filename tokens divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, two frames ~600 ms apart (typical at ~1.6 fps effective rate for 51
frames over ~30 s) produce num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model associates high num_gap values
with cross-scene pairs (since same-scene non-video pairs often have small sequential
numeric indices), so it ranks these temporally distant video frame pairs with low
same-scene probability and excludes them from the TOPK=20 selection. This leaves
many video frames with no valid SIFT neighbors and strands them as outliers with zero
cameras registered — the failure mode observed in V9 (14 stairs outliers, 6/51 total).
V12 tried to patch this with temporal augmentation, but hurt scores (4/51) because
stairs has two simultaneous cameras whose timestamps interleave: adding ±2 sequential
temporal neighbors connects frames across camera streams, creating erroneous cross-stream
edges that collapse the two sub-scenes into one unresolvable cluster. V13 instead bypasses
the LGEU model entirely for video sequences and ranks pairs by raw cosine similarity of
global descriptors (top-20 per image). Cosine similarity captures visual appearance
directly without the timestamp artifact, is the natural distance metric the global
descriptor was trained under, and is robust to the dual-camera interleaved-timestamp
structure because it scores pairs by what they look like, not when they were captured.
Non-video datasets (ETs and all hidden structured-capture datasets) are unaffected and
use the standard V9 GBM+TOPK=20 path. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs and
a net increase in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).

### V14 — regression
Hypothesis: V14 Hypothesis: For video sequences (N > 40, ≥70% timestamp filenames), the GBM LGEU model's
num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair selection. num_gap is computed as the minimum
absolute numeric token difference between two filenames divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, adjacent frames ~600 ms apart yield num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model was trained on
non-video datasets where same-scene pairs have small sequential file-index gaps (num_gap ≈ 0) and
cross-scene pairs have large gaps, so it learned to use high num_gap as a strong cross-scene signal.
Applied to video, this is a spurious artifact: the model assigns low same-scene probability to
temporally adjacent but spatially overlapping frames, causing them to miss the TOPK=20 cut and
end up as 'outliers' with no SIFT edges and zero cameras registered — exactly the failure mode
seen in V9 (14/51 stairs images stranded as outliers). Previous attempts to fix this were:
V12 (add ±2 temporal neighbor pairs), which hurt because stairs has two interleaved camera streams
whose cross-stream temporal neighbors are from different viewpoints; and V13 (bypass GBM entirely,
use cosine-only ranking), which crashed. V14 takes a more surgical approach: it runs the full GBM
model but zeros out num_gap before scoring for video sequences. This neutralizes the timestamp
artifact while preserving all six remaining discriminative features — cosine similarity, mutual
rank mean, mutual rank max, aspect_diff, area_ratio, and name_jaccard — which are all valid for
video and can distinguish same-scene from cross-scene pairs by visual appearance alone. Non-video
and small datasets (N ≤ 40) are unaffected and use standard V9 logic. We expect fewer outlier
images for stairs (and any other hidden video datasets), better scene coverage, and a net increase
in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).
Result: total=25/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=6/51

### V15 — regression
Hypothesis: V15 Hypothesis: In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images are stranded as "outliers"
with 0 cameras registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40) with
forward-facing staircase motion; consecutive frames have limited visual overlap, so many
valid same-scene pairs produce only 30-39 Lowe-ratio matches — just below the standard
GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD of 40. These pairs would survive MAGSAC geometric verification
(inliers >= MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3, confirming consistent camera geometry), but are blocked
from forming edges purely by the Lowe count cutoff, leaving their images with no valid
graph edges and thus stranded as outliers before COLMAP is even invoked. Previous fixes
(V12 temporal augmentation, V13 cosine-only ranking, V14 num_gap zeroing) all targeted
pair selection quality but left the threshold at 40, so the core cutoff problem remained.
V15 lowers good_match_threshold from 40 to 30 specifically for video sequences (N >
MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N, >=70% timestamp filenames), where LGEU pair pre-selection (TOPK=20)
already limits the candidate set to the most visually similar pairs, making cross-scene
false positives unlikely, and MAGSAC (MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3) provides a final geometric
guard. Non-video and small datasets use the unchanged V9 logic (threshold=40, TOPK=20).
We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and any hidden video datasets), more registered
cameras overall, and no regression in ETs or non-video datasets.
Result: total=26/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=7/51

Dataset: ETs (22 images)
  scene_0: 10/11 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  
  scene_1: 9/9 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  
  outliers: 0/2 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction

Dataset: stairs (51 images)
  scene_0: 3/15 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  
  scene_1: 0/2 cameras  video=False  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction
  outliers: 0/14 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  no_reconstruction
  scene_2: 3/20 cameras  video=True  match=exhaustive  

## scene_clustering.py — key patterns
### discover_scenes_v6 (base function — all versions delegate here):
def discover_scenes_v6(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    min_magsac_inliers: int = MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    lgeu_topk: int = LGEU_TOPK,
    max_exhaustive_n: int = MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """v6 scene discovery: same as v5 with fallbacks for all failure modes.

    Differences from discover_scenes_v5:
    - Per-image SIFT extraction wrapped in try/except; failed images get None features.
    - LGEU pair selection wrapped in try/except; failure falls back to exhaustive pairs.
    - _match_pair wrapped in try/except per pair; failure counts as (0, 0).
    - Guard against empty pairs_to_match list.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    if len(names) < 2:
        return {n: "scene_0" for n in names}, {}

    if verbose:
        print(f"  [v6] extracting SIFT features for {len(names)} images ...")
    features: dict[str, Optional[_SiftFeatures]] = {}
    for name in names:
        try:
            features[name] = _extract_sift(image_paths[name])
        except Exception:
            features[name] = None

    # Select which pairs to SIFT-match
    use_lgeu = lgeu_model_payload is not None and len(names) > max_exhaustive_n
    if use_lgeu:
        try:
            pairs_to_match = _lgeu_select_pairs(
                image_paths, names, lgeu_model_payload, lgeu_topk, verbose
            )
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU selected {len(pairs_to_match)} / {len(names) * (len(names) - 1) // 2} pairs")
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU pair selection failed ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); falling back to exhaustive")
            pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))
    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v15 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v15(
    image_paths,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v15: Lower Lowe threshold (30) for video sequences on the LGEU path.

    In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 stairs images end up as outliers with 0 cameras registered.
    Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N) with forward-facing staircase
    motion; frames have limited visual overlap, so some valid same-scene pairs produce
    only 30-39 Lowe matches — just below the standard threshold of 40. These pairs are
    geometrically consistent (MAGSAC-verifiable) but are blocked from forming edges by
    the Lowe count cutoff, stranding their images as outliers.

    V15 lowers good_match_threshold to 30 for video sequences (N > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    >=70% timestamp filenames). The LGEU pair pre-selection (TOPK=20) limits candidate
    pairs to the most visually similar ones, reducing cross-scene false-positive risk.
    MAGSAC geometric verification (MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3) guards against any remaining
    cross-scene pairs that happen to produce >= 30 Lowe matches. Non-video and small
    datasets use the standard V9 path (threshold=40, TOPK=20), unaffected.

    Unlike V11, which lowered the threshold for small exhaustive-path datasets (ETs)
    and caused cross-scene merging, V15 only lowers the threshold for large video
    datasets where LGEU pre-selection already constrains the candidate set.
    """
    import re as _re

    names = sorted(image_paths)
    N = len(names)


## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v11 SfM identical to v7 — uses lower Lowe threshold in scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v11 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

## submission.py — build_submission_v9 signature (template)
def build_submission_v9(
    sample_path: Path,

    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    diagnostics_path: Path | None = None,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """v9 pipeline: GBM LGEU model (AUC 0.690) + LGEU_TOPK=20 (V7 conservative pairing).

    V8 used TOPK=30 which over-merged stairs sub-scenes, regressing from 27/73 (V7)
    to 22/73. V9 reverts to TOPK=20 while keeping GBM model quality for better pair
    ranking on hidden datasets.
    """

    out_rows: list[dict] = []
    diagnostics: dict = {"datasets": {}, "version": 9}

    for dataset, group in sample.groupby("dataset", sort=False):
        image_paths: dict[str, Path] = {}
        scene_map: dict[str, str] = {}
        poses: dict[str, tuple] = {}
        sfm_info: dict = {}

            for row in group.itertuples(index=False):
                path = image_root / "test" / dataset / row.image
                if path.is_file():
                    image_paths[row.image] = path

            if len(image_paths) >= 2:
                scene_map, _edge_data = discover_scenes_v9(
                    image_paths,
                    lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
                    verbose=verbose,
                )
                poses, sfm_info = run_sfm_per_scene_v9(
                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}
            poses = {}
            sfm_info = {"error":

## Recent Experiments (full details)

### V11 — regression
Hypothesis: Lower Lowe threshold (30 vs 40) for exhaustive-path datasets (N<=40). ETs has 2 outlier images with 30-39 matches; lowering threshold may connect them to scene_0 for COLMAP registration.
Result: total=16/73, ETs=10/22, stairs=6/51

### V12 — regression
Hypothesis: V12 Hypothesis: Video-aware temporal pair augmentation for scene clustering.

In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images end up in "outliers" with 0 cameras
registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40), so LGEU pair
selection is used instead of exhaustive SIFT. LGEU scores pairs by global descriptor
similarity and selects the top-20 neighbors per image; however, for a simultaneous
dual-camera video recording with overlapping timestamps, the LGEU model may misjudge
visual similarity and fail to select the temporally adjacent frames that provide the
strongest geometric overlap. When a frame's key neighbors are not among the LGEU-selected
pairs, that frame gets no valid SIFT edges and is stranded as an outlier — preventing
COLMAP from registering it. V12 supplements the LGEU-selected pairs with mandatory
sequential temporal neighbor pairs (±2 frames sorted by embedded filename timestamp) for
all video sequences (>=70% of filenames contain a 10-13 digit timestamp). This guarantees
each frame connects to its 1-2 nearest temporal neighbors before SIFT matching, regardless
of LGEU ranking. The extra pairs add at most 2*(N-1) additional SIFT comparisons (~88 for
stairs), a modest cost. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and potentially other
video hidden datasets) and a net increase in registered cameras over V9 without regressing
ETs or non-video datasets (which are unaffected by the new logic).
Result: total=23/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=4/51

### V13 — smoke_error
Hypothesis: V13 Hypothesis: The LGEU GBM model's num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair
selection for video sequences like stairs. num_gap is computed as the minimum absolute
numeric difference between filename tokens divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, two frames ~600 ms apart (typical at ~1.6 fps effective rate for 51
frames over ~30 s) produce num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model associates high num_gap values
with cross-scene pairs (since same-scene non-video pairs often have small sequential
numeric indices), so it ranks these temporally distant video frame pairs with low
same-scene probability and excludes them from the TOPK=20 selection. This leaves
many video frames with no valid SIFT neighbors and strands them as outliers with zero
cameras registered — the failure mode observed in V9 (14 stairs outliers, 6/51 total).
V12 tried to patch this with temporal augmentation, but hurt scores (4/51) because
stairs has two simultaneous cameras whose timestamps interleave: adding ±2 sequential
temporal neighbors connects frames across camera streams, creating erroneous cross-stream
edges that collapse the two sub-scenes into one unresolvable cluster. V13 instead bypasses
the LGEU model entirely for video sequences and ranks pairs by raw cosine similarity of
global descriptors (top-20 per image). Cosine similarity captures visual appearance
directly without the timestamp artifact, is the natural distance metric the global
descriptor was trained under, and is robust to the dual-camera interleaved-timestamp
structure because it scores pairs by what they look like, not when they were captured.
Non-video datasets (ETs and all hidden structured-capture datasets) are unaffected and
use the standard V9 GBM+TOPK=20 path. We expect fewer outlier images for stairs and
a net increase in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).

### V14 — regression
Hypothesis: V14 Hypothesis: For video sequences (N > 40, ≥70% timestamp filenames), the GBM LGEU model's
num_gap feature is the root cause of poor pair selection. num_gap is computed as the minimum
absolute numeric token difference between two filenames divided by 1000; for 13-digit millisecond
Unix timestamps, adjacent frames ~600 ms apart yield num_gap ≈ 0.6. The GBM model was trained on
non-video datasets where same-scene pairs have small sequential file-index gaps (num_gap ≈ 0) and
cross-scene pairs have large gaps, so it learned to use high num_gap as a strong cross-scene signal.
Applied to video, this is a spurious artifact: the model assigns low same-scene probability to
temporally adjacent but spatially overlapping frames, causing them to miss the TOPK=20 cut and
end up as 'outliers' with no SIFT edges and zero cameras registered — exactly the failure mode
seen in V9 (14/51 stairs images stranded as outliers). Previous attempts to fix this were:
V12 (add ±2 temporal neighbor pairs), which hurt because stairs has two interleaved camera streams
whose cross-stream temporal neighbors are from different viewpoints; and V13 (bypass GBM entirely,
use cosine-only ranking), which crashed. V14 takes a more surgical approach: it runs the full GBM
model but zeros out num_gap before scoring for video sequences. This neutralizes the timestamp
artifact while preserving all six remaining discriminative features — cosine similarity, mutual
rank mean, mutual rank max, aspect_diff, area_ratio, and name_jaccard — which are all valid for
video and can distinguish same-scene from cross-scene pairs by visual appearance alone. Non-video
and small datasets (N ≤ 40) are unaffected and use standard V9 logic. We expect fewer outlier
images for stairs (and any other hidden video datasets), better scene coverage, and a net increase
in registered cameras over V9 (25/73) without regressing ETs (19/22).
Result: total=25/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=6/51

### V15 — regression
Hypothesis: V15 Hypothesis: In V9 (GBM+TOPK=20), 14 of 51 stairs images are stranded as "outliers"
with 0 cameras registered. Stairs is a video sequence (N=51 > MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N=40) with
forward-facing staircase motion; consecutive frames have limited visual overlap, so many
valid same-scene pairs produce only 30-39 Lowe-ratio matches — just below the standard
GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD of 40. These pairs would survive MAGSAC geometric verification
(inliers >= MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3, confirming consistent camera geometry), but are blocked
from forming edges purely by the Lowe count cutoff, leaving their images with no valid
graph edges and thus stranded as outliers before COLMAP is even invoked. Previous fixes
(V12 temporal augmentation, V13 cosine-only ranking, V14 num_gap zeroing) all targeted
pair selection quality but left the threshold at 40, so the core cutoff problem remained.
V15 lowers good_match_threshold from 40 to 30 specifically for video sequences (N >
MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N, >=70% timestamp filenames), where LGEU pair pre-selection (TOPK=20)
already limits the candidate set to the most visually similar pairs, making cross-scene
false positives unlikely, and MAGSAC (MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3) provides a final geometric
guard. Non-video and small datasets use the unchanged V9 logic (threshold=40, TOPK=20).
We expect fewer outlier images for stairs (and any hidden video datasets), more registered
cameras overall, and no regression in ETs or non-video datasets.
Result: total=26/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=7/51

### V16 — regression
Hypothesis: V16 Hypothesis: V14 and V15 each targeted a distinct bottleneck for video sequences like
stairs but never in combination. V14 zeroed the GBM model's num_gap feature before scoring
(neutralising the timestamp artifact that causes the model to treat temporally-adjacent
same-scene frames as cross-scene), but kept good_match_threshold=40 — result: 6/51 stairs,
identical to V9, because the improved pair selection still blocked weak-overlap edges. V15
lowered good_match_threshold to 30 for video sequences (admitting pairs with 30–39 Lowe
matches that pass MAGSAC geometric verification), but left num_gap active — result: 7/51
stairs, a marginal gain, because the biased GBM model still ranked some valid pairs below
the TOPK=20 cut. V16 combines both changes simultaneously for video sequences (N >
MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N, >=70% timestamp filenames): the GBM model scores all pairs with num_gap
forced to 0.0, producing a ranking driven purely by visual similarity (cosine, mutual rank,
aspect, area ratio, name overlap), and edges are admitted at a Lowe count of 30 rather than
40, with MAGSAC (MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3) and LGEU TOPK=20 still guarding against cross-scene
merging. Because these two fixes are orthogonal — one improves WHICH pairs are selected, the
other lowers the bar for FORMING edges among selected pairs — their combination should reduce
both the number of outlier images and the number of frames that are correctly clustered but
fail to form valid SIFT edges, yielding a net increase in registered stairs cameras over
V15 (7/51) without regressing ETs or non-video hidden datasets (which are unaffected by
the video-path changes).
Result: total=26/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=7/51

## scene_clustering.py — key patterns
### discover_scenes_v6 (base function — all versions delegate here):
def discover_scenes_v6(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    min_magsac_inliers: int = MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    lgeu_topk: int = LGEU_TOPK,
    max_exhaustive_n: int = MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """v6 scene discovery: same as v5 with fallbacks for all failure modes.

    Differences from discover_scenes_v5:
    - Per-image SIFT extraction wrapped in try/except; failed images get None features.
    - LGEU pair selection wrapped in try/except; failure falls back to exhaustive pairs.
    - _match_pair wrapped in try/except per pair; failure counts as (0, 0).
    - Guard against empty pairs_to_match list.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    if len(names) < 2:
        return {n: "scene_0" for n in names}, {}

    if verbose:
        print(f"  [v6] extracting SIFT features for {len(names)} images ...")
    features: dict[str, Optional[_SiftFeatures]] = {}
    for name in names:
        try:
            features[name] = _extract_sift(image_paths[name])
        except Exception:
            features[name] = None

    # Select which pairs to SIFT-match
    use_lgeu = lgeu_model_payload is not None and len(names) > max_exhaustive_n
    if use_lgeu:
        try:
            pairs_to_match = _lgeu_select_pairs(
                image_paths, names, lgeu_model_payload, lgeu_topk, verbose
            )
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU selected {len(pairs_to_match)} / {len(names) * (len(names) - 1) // 2} pairs")
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU pair selection failed ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); falling back to exhaustive")
            pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))
    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v16 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v16(
    image_paths,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v16: GBM LGEU with num_gap=0 (V14) combined with Lowe threshold=30 (V15) for video.

    V14 zeroed num_gap before GBM scoring for video sequences, neutralising the timestamp
    artifact that causes the model to rank temporally-adjacent same-scene frames low.
    V14 result: 6/51 stairs (same as V9 — the improved pair selection was offset by the
    40-match Lowe cutoff still blocking weak-but-valid edges).

    V15 lowered good_match_threshold to 30 for video sequences, admitting pairs with
    30-39 Lowe matches that are geometrically consistent (MAGSAC-verifiable). V15 result:
    7/51 stairs — better pair admission helped but pair selection still used the biased
    num_gap feature.

    V16 combines both changes for video sequences:
    1. GBM scoring with num_gap=0 → selects pairs by visual appearance, not timestamp gaps
    2. good_match_threshold=30 → admits weak-overlap but geometrically valid same-scene edges
    MAGSAC (MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS=3) and LGEU TOPK=20 still guard against cross-scene merging.

    Non-video and small datasets (N <= MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N) use standard V9 logic (full GBM,
    num_gap active, threshold=40, TOPK=20), unaffected.
    """
    import re as _re

    import numpy as np

    names = sorted(image_paths)
    N = len(names)

    _ts_re = _re.compile(r"\d{10,13}")
    n_match = sum(1 for n in names if _ts_re.search(n))
    is_video = N >= 3 and 

## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v11 SfM identical to v7 — uses lower Lowe threshold in scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v11 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

                    image_paths[row.image] = path

                )

                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}

            sfm_info = {"error":
