## Recent Experiments (full details)

### V20 — regression
Hypothesis: V20 Hypothesis: Versions V14-V19 all tried to rescue the 14 outlier stairs images
by forcing SIFT edges at the Python level — through GBM num_gap neutralisation (V14),
lower Lowe-match thresholds (V15/V16), looser Lowe ratios (V19), and increased SIFT
feature density (V17). None succeeded: these 14 images cannot accumulate even 30 Lowe
matches with any of their top-20 GBM-selected neighbours under any tested parametrisation.
The SIFT edge gate is simply impassable for them. V20 abandons the SIFT-edge rescue
approach entirely and instead uses post-clustering outlier reassignment: after V16
clustering (GBM num_gap=0 + Lowe threshold=30) assigns images to scenes or "outliers",
each outlier is reassigned to the scene of its most visually similar non-outlier image
by cosine similarity of precomputed global descriptors. These outlier images are then
passed to COLMAP as members of their reassigned scene (scene_0 or scene_2). COLMAP
runs exhaustive matching on the enlarged scene using its own internal SIFT pipeline —
without the 30-match Python threshold — giving the outlier images a genuine second
chance at registration. If the outlier images share even sparse geometric overlap with
their reassigned scene neighbours, COLMAP's bundle adjustment can register them. Worst
case, they fail to register and receive identity poses, which is no worse than their
current status. SfM uses the proven V7 path (exhaustive for N<=100 video) to avoid
the upload failures seen in V18/V19. Non-video and small datasets (N<=40) are fully
unaffected and use standard V9 logic throughout.
Result: total=24/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=5/51

### V21 — tied
Hypothesis: V21 Hypothesis: Versions V7-V20 (excluding the upload_failed V18/V19) use
abs_pose_min_num_inliers=4 in COLMAP's incremental mapper for video scenes. For
forward-facing staircase video, COLMAP initializes from a seed pair and attempts to
extend the reconstruction frame by frame. Consecutive staircase frames have limited
visual overlap (small inter-frame baseline) and the resulting sparse 3D point cloud
contains very few points visible in any single adjacent frame — often exactly 3, the
theoretical P3P minimum. With the threshold at 4, COLMAP rejects these frames as
unregisterable, which explains why scene_0 (15 images) and scene_2 (20 images) both
plateau at only 3 cameras each in V9 diagnostics despite having real geometric overlap.
V21 lowers abs_pose_min_num_inliers from 4 to 3 (the P3P minimum) for video scenes
only, allowing cameras with sparse-but-real 3D correspondences to be registered. V18
changed three mapper parameters simultaneously (init_min_num_inliers, init_min_tri_angle,
abs_pose_min_num_inliers) and upload_failed; V21 isolates only abs_pose_min_num_inliers
to cleanly attribute any effect and reduce the risk of API-level failures. Scene
clustering uses V16 (GBM num_gap=0 + Lowe threshold=30 for video), the best-scoring
clustering configuration (7/51 stairs, 19/22 ETs). Non-video scenes and small datasets
(N<=40) retain V7's original settings (abs_pose_min_num_inliers=8) unchanged, protecting
ETs and hidden structured-capture datasets from regression. Expected improvement: 2-4
additional stairs cameras registered within scene_0 and scene_2, potentially exceeding
V7's best of 8/51 stairs cameras.
Result: total=27/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=8/51

### V22 — regression
Hypothesis: V22 Hypothesis: V21 lowered abs_pose_min_num_inliers from 4 to 3 (the theoretical P3P
minimum) for video scenes and tied V7 at 8/51 stairs cameras registered. The remaining
untried COLMAP mapper parameter from V18 — which changed three parameters simultaneously
and upload_failed — is init_min_tri_angle. V21 preserves the V7 video default of 1.0
degrees for this parameter. For a forward-facing staircase recording, consecutive frames
share a narrow field of view and nearly-parallel optical axes; the true geometric baseline
between adjacent frames is small, so the triangulation angle of any candidate seed pair
often falls below 1.0 degrees. When COLMAP's incremental mapper evaluates seed pairs for
initialisation, it rejects those whose triangulation angle is below init_min_tri_angle,
forcing it to select a geometrically inferior seed pair — one with a wider apparent angle
but weaker photometric overlap. Starting from a poor seed pair produces a sparse initial
3D point cloud, which in turn limits how many subsequent frames can accumulate the three
inlier correspondences required for registration (even with abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3).
V22 lowers init_min_tri_angle from 1.0 to 0.5 degrees for video scenes only, allowing
COLMAP to select the geometrically best available seed pair. A better seed pair yields a
denser initial point cloud, which increases the likelihood that adjacent frames find three
or more inlier correspondences and can be registered. Combined with V21's
abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3, this targets the seed-initialisation bottleneck that V21 alone
could not address. Non-video scenes and small datasets (N <= MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N) retain V7
settings unchanged, so ETs and hidden structured-capture datasets are unaffected.
Result: total=26/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=7/51

### V23 — smoke_error
Hypothesis: V23 Hypothesis: Use pycolmap.CameraMode.SINGLE instead of CameraMode.AUTO for video scenes in COLMAP feature extraction.

All prior versions V7–V22 call pycolmap.extract_features(..., camera_mode=pycolmap.CameraMode.AUTO), which assigns an independent camera model (SIMPLE_RADIAL by default) to every individual image. For forward-facing staircase video, all frames come from the same physical camera with a fixed focal length and principal point that do not change between frames. Under AUTO mode, COLMAP must estimate intrinsics separately for each image from the matches available at that point in the incremental reconstruction; for scenes of only 15–20 images with a sparse initial point cloud, these per-image estimates are poorly constrained and inconsistent across frames, producing depth ambiguities that reduce the number of well-triangulated 3D points visible in each candidate frame. V21 improved registration by lowering abs_pose_min_num_inliers from 4 to 3 (P3P minimum), allowing frames with only 3 matching 3D points to register; V22 added init_min_tri_angle=0.5 degrees and regressed (8→7 stairs cameras), so V23 reverts to V21's init_min_tri_angle=1.0. The new change is CameraMode.SINGLE: COLMAP shares one camera model across all images in the scene and jointly optimises its intrinsics during every bundle adjustment round. Joint optimisation across 15–20 frames provides far more constraints on focal length and principal point than any single seed pair, yielding a more accurately calibrated camera model and geometrically tighter 3D point positions. Tighter 3D points increase the fraction of adjacent frames that can accumulate the 3 inlier correspondences required for registration, directly attacking the bottleneck (scene_0: 3–5/15, scene_2: 3/20 registered cameras) that has limited stairs performance in all previous versions. Non-video scenes retain CameraMode.AUTO and V7 mapper settings, preserving ETs and hidden structured-capture dataset performance.

### V24 — smoke_error
Hypothesis: V24 Hypothesis: Isolate init_min_num_inliers reduction (5->3) combined with V21's
abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3 for video scenes, without touching init_min_tri_angle.

V18 changed three COLMAP mapper parameters simultaneously for video scenes —
init_min_num_inliers (5->3), init_min_tri_angle (1.0->0.5 degrees), and
abs_pose_min_num_inliers (4->3) — and upload_failed, leaving all three parameters
untested as individual contributions. V21 isolated abs_pose_min_num_inliers (4->3) alone
and tied V7 at 8/51 stairs cameras (27/73 total). V22 isolated init_min_tri_angle (1.0->0.5
degrees) on top of V21's abs_pose=3 and regressed to 7/51 stairs cameras (26/73).
The third parameter from V18's bundle — init_min_num_inliers — has never been tested
in isolation. This parameter controls the minimum number of inliers required for a seed
pair to be accepted during COLMAP initialization. V7 sets it to 5 for video scenes; for
staircase video with nearly-parallel optical axes and a shallow scene extent, even the
geometrically best available seed pair may produce only 3–5 verified fundamental matrix
inliers (the COLMAP sparse exhaustive matching still runs, but the H/F verification step
yields few geometric inliers when both cameras nearly share a focus). With
init_min_num_inliers=5, COLMAP silently discards these seed-pair candidates and either
selects a geometrically worse seed pair — producing a poor initial 3D point cloud — or
fails to initialize entirely, explaining why scene_0 (15 images) and scene_2 (20 images)
sometimes plateau at only 3 registered cameras even with abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3.
V24 reduces init_min_num_inliers from 5 to 3 (matching the P3P minimum), combined with
V21's abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3, while explicitly keeping init_min_tri_angle at 1.0
degrees (V22 showed that lowering it causes regression). Scene clustering inherits V16
(GBM num_gap=0 + Lowe threshold=30 for video, best proven configuration). Non-video and
small datasets (N<=40) retain V7 settings (init_min_num_inliers=10, abs_pose=8),
protecting ETs and hidden structured-capture datasets from regression. Expected outcome:
allowing COLMAP to initialize from seed pairs with 3–4 geometric inliers should yield a
more accurately positioned initial 3D point cloud, enabling more adjacent staircase frames
to find the 3 inlier correspondences required by abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3, potentially
recovering additional cameras beyond V21's 8/51 stairs result.

### V25 — tied
Hypothesis: V25 Hypothesis: All prior versions V7–V24 call pycolmap.incremental_mapping() and then
take ONLY the largest sub-reconstruction with:
    best_rec = max(reconstructions.values(), key=lambda r: r.num_reg_images())
This silently discards every smaller sub-reconstruction that COLMAP produced. For the
stairs dataset, two physically separate camera rigs record simultaneously from different
positions. COLMAP's incremental mapper naturally produces multiple disjoint
sub-reconstructions — one per rig cluster — because the two rigs share no geometric
overlap and cannot be merged into a single model. The V9 diagnostics confirm this
structure: scene_0 (15 images, 3 cameras registered in V9) and scene_2 (20 images,
3 cameras) are two separate video scenes; V21 (the current best at 8/51) reaches 8
cameras by lowering abs_pose_min_num_inliers to 3. However, even within each scene,
COLMAP likely produces additional smaller sub-reconstructions (e.g., two isolated
temporal clusters within scene_0's 15 images that could not be bridged) that are
currently discarded. V25 merges poses from ALL sub-reconstructions returned by
incremental_mapping, sorting by descending registration count so that for any image
appearing in multiple sub-reconstructions, the pose from the largest (most globally
constrained) reconstruction wins. This approach does not change any matching or mapper
threshold — it simply recovers cameras that COLMAP correctly reconstructed but that
were being silently dropped. Scene clustering is unchanged from V21/V16 (GBM num_gap=0
+ Lowe threshold=30 for video, TOPK=20). Mapper settings are identical to V21
(abs_pose_min_num_inliers=3 for video, init_min_tri_angle=1.0, init_min_num_inliers=5).
Non-video and small datasets (N<=40) are fully unaffected — their exhaustive path
typically produces a single reconstruction, so merging all reconstructions is a no-op.
Expected outcome: if COLMAP produces 2+ sub-reconstructions per video scene (a plausible
scenario for dual-rig staircase recording), V25 could recover the cameras from secondary
sub-reconstructions, potentially exceeding V21's 8/51 stairs result.
Result: total=27/73, ETs=19/22, stairs=8/51

## scene_clustering.py — key patterns
### discover_scenes_v6 (base function — all versions delegate here):
def discover_scenes_v6(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    good_match_threshold: int = GOOD_MATCH_THRESHOLD,
    min_magsac_inliers: int = MIN_MAGSAC_INLIERS,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    lgeu_topk: int = LGEU_TOPK,
    max_exhaustive_n: int = MAX_EXHAUSTIVE_N,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[tuple[str, str], tuple[int, int]]]:
    """v6 scene discovery: same as v5 with fallbacks for all failure modes.

    Differences from discover_scenes_v5:
    - Per-image SIFT extraction wrapped in try/except; failed images get None features.
    - LGEU pair selection wrapped in try/except; failure falls back to exhaustive pairs.
    - _match_pair wrapped in try/except per pair; failure counts as (0, 0).
    - Guard against empty pairs_to_match list.
    """
    names = sorted(image_paths)
    if len(names) < 2:
        return {n: "scene_0" for n in names}, {}

    if verbose:
        print(f"  [v6] extracting SIFT features for {len(names)} images ...")
    features: dict[str, Optional[_SiftFeatures]] = {}
    for name in names:
        try:
            features[name] = _extract_sift(image_paths[name])
        except Exception:
            features[name] = None

    # Select which pairs to SIFT-match
    use_lgeu = lgeu_model_payload is not None and len(names) > max_exhaustive_n
    if use_lgeu:
        try:
            pairs_to_match = _lgeu_select_pairs(
                image_paths, names, lgeu_model_payload, lgeu_topk, verbose
            )
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU selected {len(pairs_to_match)} / {len(names) * (len(names) - 1) // 2} pairs")
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v6] LGEU pair selection failed ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}); falling back to exhaustive")
            pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))
    else:
        pairs_to_match = list(itertools.combinations(names, 2))

    if not pairs_to_match:
        retur

### discover_scenes_v25 (most recent version):
def discover_scenes_v25(
    image_paths,
    lgeu_model_payload=None,
    verbose=False,
):
    """v25: V16 clustering unchanged. V25's improvement is merging ALL COLMAP reconstructions.

    All prior versions V7–V24 call pycolmap.incremental_mapping() and then take only the
    LARGEST reconstruction:
        best_rec = max(reconstructions.values(), key=lambda r: r.num_reg_images())
    This silently discards every smaller sub-reconstruction. For staircase video recorded
    simultaneously by two physically separate camera rigs, COLMAP's incremental mapper
    naturally produces multiple disjoint sub-reconstructions — one per rig cluster — because
    the two rigs share no geometric overlap and cannot be merged into a single model. With
    N=35 images across scene_0 (15) and scene_2 (20), if COLMAP produces reconstructions of
    sizes [5, 4] for scene_0, the current code registers only 5 cameras and silently drops 4.

    V25 fixes this by merging poses from ALL reconstructions returned by incremental_mapping,
    sorted by descending registration count. For any image registered in multiple
    sub-reconstructions (a rare corner case), the pose from the largest reconstruction wins.
    This is the first version to exploit sub-reconstructions and directly targets the
    observation that V21 (the best version at 8/51) can only use one sub-reconstruction per
    scene despite COLMAP often finding more.

    Scene clustering uses V16 (GBM num_gap=0 + Lowe threshold=30 for v

## sfm.py — run_sfm_per_scene_v7 signature
def run_sfm_per_scene_v7(
    image_paths: dict[str, Path],
    scene_map: dict[str, str],
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]], dict]:
    """v7 per-scene SfM: uses run_sfm_v7; catches any exception per scene."""
    from collections import defaultdict

    scene_groups: dict[str, dict[str, Path]] = defaultdict(dict)
    for name, label in scene_map.items():
        if name in image_paths:
            scene_groups[label][name] = image_paths[name]

    all_poses: dict[str, tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]] = {}
    all_info: dict[str, dict] = {}

    for label, group_paths in scene_groups.items():
        if verbose:
            print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}': {len(group_paths)} images")
        try:
            scene_poses, scene_info = run_sfm_v7(group_paths, verbose=verbose)
        except Exception as exc:
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [SfM-v7] scene '{label}' threw {type(exc).__name__}; using identity")
            scene_poses = {n: _identity_pose() for n in group_paths}
            scene_info = {
                "error": f"{type(exc).__name__}",
                "registered": 0,
                "total": len(group_paths),
            }
        all_poses.update(scene_poses)
        all_info[label] = scene_info

    for name in image_paths:
        if name not in all_poses:
            all_poses[name] = _identity_pose()

    return all_poses, all_info


# v9 SfM is identical to v7 — improvements are in scene clustering (TOPK=20)
run_sfm_per_scene_v9 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v10 SfM identical to v7 — uses TOPK=25 scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v10 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v11 SfM identical to v7 — uses lower Lowe threshold in scene clustering; SfM unchanged
run_sfm_per_scene_v11 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

# v12 SfM identical to v7 — uses video-aware temporal pair augmentation in scene clustering
run_sfm_per_scene_v12 = run_sfm_per_scene_v7

## submission.py — build_submission_v9 signature (template)
def build_submission_v9(
    sample_path: Path,
    image_root: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    lgeu_model_payload: dict | None = None,
    diagnostics_path: Path | None = None,
    verbose: bool = False,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """v9 pipeline: GBM LGEU model (AUC 0.690) + LGEU_TOPK=20 (V7 conservative pairing).

    V8 used TOPK=30 which over-merged stairs sub-scenes, regressing from 27/73 (V7)
    to 22/73. V9 reverts to TOPK=20 while keeping GBM model quality for better pair
    ranking on hidden datasets.
    """
    sample = read_sample(sample_path)
    out_rows: list[dict] = []
    diagnostics: dict = {"datasets": {}, "version": 9}

    for dataset, group in sample.groupby("dataset", sort=False):
        image_paths: dict[str, Path] = {}
        scene_map: dict[str, str] = {}
        poses: dict[str, tuple] = {}
        sfm_info: dict = {}
        dataset_error: str | None = None

        try:
            for row in group.itertuples(index=False):
                path = image_root / "test" / dataset / row.image
                if path.is_file():
                    image_paths[row.image] = path

            if len(image_paths) >= 2:
                scene_map, _edge_data = discover_scenes_v9(
                    image_paths,
                    lgeu_model_payload=lgeu_model_payload,
                    verbose=verbose,
                )
                poses, sfm_info = run_sfm_per_scene_v9(
                    image_paths, scene_map, verbose=verbose
                )
            else:
                scene_map = {img: "scene_0" for img in image_paths}
                poses = {img: identity_pose() for img in image_paths}
                sfm_info = {}

        except Exception as exc:
            dataset_error = f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            if verbose:
                print(f"  [v9] ERROR in dataset '{dataset}': {dataset_error}")
                traceback.print_exc()
            scene_map = {}
            poses = {}
            sfm_info = {"error": 

## Dataset Context
Test data structure (at Kaggle evaluation time):
  ETs:   22 images, non-video, exhaustive SIFT matching.
         V9 diagnostics: scene_0 (10/11), scene_1 (9/9), outliers (0/2 — no_reconstruction).
         The 2 outlier images have < 40 Lowe matches with both main scenes.

  stairs: 51 images total, is_video=True for most scenes.
          Two simultaneous multi-camera recordings with OVERLAPPING timestamps.
          V9 diagnostics: scene_0 (3/15 video), scene_1 (0/2 no_recon),
                          outliers (0/14 video no_recon!), scene_2 (3/20 video).
          V7 (LR model, TOPK=20): 8/51 — scene_0 5/15, scene_2 3/20.
          V9 (GBM model, TOPK=20): 6/51 — GBM selects different pairs than LR.
          14 images end up in 'outliers' with no reconstruction at all.

Other test datasets (hidden — not accessible locally):
  amy_gardens (200 imgs), fbk_vineyard (163), imc2023_haiper (54),
  imc2023_heritage (209), urban_country (113), ... etc.
  These are the primary source of the remaining 73 - 22 - 51 = 0 cameras
  (i.e., ALL public cameras come from ETs + stairs).
  Improvement must target ETs and/or stairs registration without breaking other datasets.
